# MARION-IA-USA — Mixed Local Model Deployment + LLM Manager Stabilization Plan (V5)

**Date:** 2026-09-23  
**Current planning time:** ~15:32 Central Time  
**Hard deadline:** **21:00 Central Time** — the environment must be back in a known-good, documented, serving state by then.  
**Primary objective:** implement the chosen mixed-model fleet, capture rollback state, perform short acceptance benchmarks, and complete the minimum LLM Manager history/benchmark work needed for safe ongoing operations.

---

# 0. Final target architecture

This plan prioritizes **implementation first**, then **short validation benchmarks**, then **LLM Manager history/restore usability**, while keeping enough rollback discipline to avoid losing the currently working state.

## Target model placement

| Host / VM | Primary local model | Runtime | Primary role |
|---|---|---|---|
| **MIAM-00111 / VM103** | **Qwen3.8-Flash-Next** | vLLM / compatible Ampere-serving environment | Smartest local agentic/coding lane |
| **MIAM-00143 / VM109** | **Gemma 4 26B-A4B** | vLLM | High-concurrency frontier worker |
| **MIAM-00144 / VM111** | **Gemma 4 26B-A4B** | vLLM | High-concurrency frontier worker / replica |
| **MIAM-00112 / VM401** | **Qwen3.6-35B-A3B** | vLLM | Proven fast worker lane |
| **MIAM-00149 / VM149** | **Qwen3.8-27B** | llama.cpp | Single-agent prototype / independent fallback |

No local model should depend on cross-node distributed inference for the initial stable state.

The two Gemma nodes should be independent replicas behind one logical LLM Manager pool.

---

# 1. Stability-first rules

1. **Do not destroy any working runtime before its exact configuration is captured.**
2. **Do not upgrade an existing custom vLLM environment in place.**
3. New experimental runtimes go into a **new versioned environment/path**.
4. Every current working configuration receives a rollback revision in LLM Manager before replacement.
5. Failed experiments are documented and hidden by default, not deleted.
6. If a new model cannot be made stable quickly, restore the known-good prior model instead of consuming the entire maintenance window.
7. At **20:15 Central**, stop beginning new experiments.
8. By **20:30 Central**, begin final rollback/stabilization if anything is still uncertain.
9. By **21:00 Central**, every host must either:
   - serve its intended model successfully, or
   - be restored to a known-good prior configuration.

---

# 2. Time budget

Approximate remaining window: **5.5 hours**.

Recommended schedule:

```text
15:32–16:00  Fresh live capture + baseline rollback/history entries
16:00–16:20  Quick pre-change benchmarks of current endpoints
16:20–16:50  LLM Manager minimum viable history/benchmark schema + API support
16:50–17:20  MIAM-00111 PCIe capture + cold power cycle + post-cycle validation
17:20–18:30  Qwen3.8-Flash-Next on MIAM-00111
17:20–18:20  Gemma 4 on MIAM-00143
18:20–19:00  Clone Gemma config to MIAM-00144 and validate
19:00–19:30  Confirm/benchmark Qwen3.6 on MIAM-00112
19:00–20:00  Qwen3.8-27B llama.cpp on MIAM-00149
20:00–20:30  LLM Manager routing/history UI + quick Hermes validation
20:30–20:45  Final benchmark pass + rollback audit
20:45–21:00  Final handoff and known-good-state verification
```

Parallel work is allowed where it does not create ambiguity or risk.

For every current local endpoint run:

```text

Prompt: ~1K tokens
Output: 128 tokens
Measured requests: 2
```

Record:

```text
TTFT
output tok/s
total latency
```

## Test B — medium request

```text
Concurrency: 1
Prompt: ~8K tokens
Output: 128 tokens
Measured requests: 1
```

## Test C — quick concurrency

GPU models:

```text
C4
then C8 if C4 succeeds
```

Stop if:

```text
OOM
engine restart
major errors
per-agent throughput <20 tok/s
```

VM149:

```text
C1 only
```

## Test D — context acceptance

For long-context production GPU models:

```text
65K–68K actual-token prompt
64 output tokens
C1
```

This is a capability test only.

Store all results in LLM Manager history.

# 7. LLM Manager availability during work

Keep VM114 available during:

```text
baseline capture
benchmark capture
rollback creation
deployment state transitions
```

Use MAINTENANCE/recovery suppression for models being manually reconfigured.

Temporarily stop LLM Manager components only if:

```text
schema migration requires exclusive access
recovery automation would interfere
partially deployed code would corrupt history
```

If stopping services:

1. stop recovery automation first;
2. perform migration/code update;
3. restart API/UI;
4. validate;
5. restart recovery automation last.

# 8. Phase 5 — MIAM-00111 PCIe check and cold power cycle

This happens **after** baseline capture + pre-change benchmark.

Before shutdown:

```bash
lspci -tv
lspci -nnk
lspci -vv
journalctl -k -b
```

Map the reported PCIe **port 5** to the real root port/BDF/device.

Capture:

```text
AER corrected errors
uncorrected errors
link retrains
Completion Timeout
BadTLP
BadDLLP
DPC
link-down events
```

Cold-cycle:

```text
MIAM-00111 only
```

Leave MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144 powered.

After boot verify:

```text
all six GPUs
Mellanox NICs
SR-IOV VF creation
VFIO bindings
VM103 passthrough
PCIe link widths/speeds
AER state
```

Run a short RDMA connectivity check against MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144.

Do not spend the entire window chasing a cosmetic corrected AER counter if the system is stable.

**V5 received — executing.** Premise check vs. this morning's live recon first:

- ✅ DeepSeek dropped entirely (it never fit — correct call)
- ✅ Flash-Next moved off VM149 (62 GB RAM, 70 GB smallest GGUF = impossible) to a GPU node, with a host-RAM gate and a 70-min time box + rollback to Qwen3.8-27B
- ✅ Gemma pool, baseline-before-destroy, and the 20:15/20:30 stability gates are all sound

**3 flags before money-time:**

1. **Flash-Next Ampere artifact-fit gate is missing.** §9 gates on host RAM but not on VRAM/artifact format. 6× RTX 3080 = 120 GB VRAM, SM86 has **no FP8 path**, and the official Q8_0 GGUF is 163 GB. vLLM needs an Ampere-loadable artifact ≤~100 GB (GPTQ/AWQ INT8/INT4). I'm checking artifact availability **now** — if none exists, this gets marked BLOCKED at minute 5, not minute 69 of the window.
2. **`qwen3.6-35b-a3b` alias currently resolves to the VM109+VM111 pool** (262K ctx, custom turboquant fork). Converting those nodes to Gemma drops that production route — I'll re-point the alias to VM401 (same model, already serving as `fast`) **before** taking 109/111 down.
3. Clock check: confirming actual Central time now — if we're past 15:32 the schedule compresses, not extends.

Starting **Phase 1 (read-only live capture)** immediately. Loading ops skills + setting up the work folder.

Heavy benchmarks (25K-context prefills) can stall the probe past timeout → false-positive `service_degraded` → bounded service restart **kills the benchmark mid-run**. Evidence: recovery_events ids 16–18 (22:48) killed a 25K leg; after the MAINTENANCE patch, identical load produced `maintenance_skip` (ids 19–24) and the bench survived.\n\nProcedure before any heavy bench:\n```sql\nUPDATE hosts SET desired_service_state='MAINTENANCE' WHERE guest_ip='<ip>';\n```\nAfterwards restore:\n```sql\nUPDATE hosts SET desired_service_state='SERVING' WHERE guest_ip='<ip>';\n```\nEngine behavior (v011_recovery.py on VM114 `/opt/llm-manager/app/`): `MAINTENANCE` → logs `maintenance_skip`, never restarts; `SERVING` → bounded recovery (2 VM starts / 2 service restarts, cooldown, then FAILED). Backups: `v011_recovery.py.bak.maint`.\n\n**Always run bench legs serially.** Two concurrent bench jobs on one host will saturate it and trigger the false-positive path even with SERVING semantics.\n\n## vllm bench serve gotchas (vLLM 0.28)\n- `--tokenizer <real HF repo>` is REQUIRED — the served alias (e.g. `qwen3.6-35b-a3b`) fails HF lookup and all requests fail with 0 completed.\n- Endpoint form: `--base-url http://127.0.0.1:8000 --endpoint /v1/chat/completions`. A full URL in `--endpoint` silently completes 0 requests with rc=0.\n- Set `HF_HOME=/opt/vllm-cache` (model/tokenizer cache location).\n- Request-rate approximates concurrency for async traffic; `--num-prompts max(C*4,32)`; 16K-input legs take ~2–4 min each, 25K ~2.5 min for 40 prompts.\n\n## Host topologies (measured)\n- **VM401 (6× RTX 5060 Ti 16G, MIAM-00112), Qwen3.6-35B-A3B AWQ MoE:** TP1/DP6/EP6 works (3B-active MoE fits per-GPU). max_model_len 32768. Production preset `FAST-WORKER-PRODUCTION` in hosting_command_presets (scope_key 10.0.20.162). Full quick matrix C1–C10 × 4K/16K all-pass, ~8.2K tok/s at 16K, soak 400/400 at C10×8K.\n- **VM103 (6× RTX 3080 20G, MIAM-00111), Qwen3.8-27B AWQ dense:** DP6 **OOMs** (each DP worker loads full 27B weights). TP6 fails `AssertionError: 16 is not divisible by 6` — attn heads 24, KV heads 4 → TP must divide 4: TP ∈ {1,2,4}. Working: **TP2/DP3 + `--max-num-seqs 128`** (linear-attention/Mamba cache only ~163 blocks at gpu-mem-util 0.90; default max_num_seqs 256 fails CUDA graph capture). TP4 single replica also works; 2 GPUs idle.\n- Qwen3.8-27B is a reasoning model: `content` may be None with text in `reasoning_content` — merge both when grading outputs (eval_coding.py does this).\n\n## Benchmark comparison method (quality-per-energy)\n1. Coding eval first (`/root/eval_coding.py` on VM103): 6 fixed tasks, rubric substring groups; compare wall-time + output tokens at equal rubric score.\n2. Then quick matrix C1/4/8/10 × 4K/16K per topology; compare aggregate tok/s AND per-GPU power draw (`nvidia-smi --query-gpu=power.draw`) — fewer active GPUs at similar quality = better energy/task.\n\n## VM401 NVIDIA per-kernel driver risk\nVM401 boots kernel 6.8.0-139 but NVIDIA modules existed only for 138 (no DKMS). GPUs vanish → vLLM dead. Fix: `apt-get install linux-modules-nvidia-580-open-<kernel>-generic`, modprobe nvidia/nvidia_modeset/nvidia_uvm, restart vllm. **Pin kernels on GPU VMs or install matching module packages before any reboot.**\n\n## Provisioning presets (hosting_command_presets on CT115 llmmanager DB)\nColumns: scope_type/scope_key/engine/friendly_name/content/**content_hash (NOT NULL, sha256 of content)**/created_by. Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n  Ready-made drain-loop runner: `scripts/ssh_run.py` (see scripts dir; handles the\n  empty/truncated `child.before` pitfall and reads the password from profile memory).\n  **Password auth REQUIRES pexpect** — `subprocess.run(..., input=PW)` fails silently\n  (Permission denied) because the password prompt appears AFTER stdin is consumed.\n  If `sshpass` is absent and uninstalled, port the loop to pexpect, never to input-feed.\n- ssh_run.py takes **IP addresses or full resolvable names, not bare node short-names**\n  (`miam00112` → \"Could not resolve hostname\"; use `10.0.20.112`).\n- **PITFALL: credential-extraction regexes coupling scripts to memory formatting.**\n  ssh_run-style scripts that parse the profile MEMORY.md for the password (`r\"root/(\\S+?)\\)\"`\n  etc.) break when memory entries are rewritten (a batch prune changed one entry's\n  phrasing and every helper died with `AssertionError: credential not found`). Make the\n  regex resilient (match `root/<word>` anywhere: `r\"root/(\\w+)\"`), or read the credential\n  from a dedicated file the scripts own — and after any memory rewrite, smoke-test one\n  helper before batch-driving the fleet.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- **PITFALL: pexpect `child.before` empty/truncated on long batched output.** A single\n  `expect(EOF)` + one `child.before` read can return EMPTY (match at buffer start) or\n  lose most output. Drain instead: loop\n  `expect([r'[Pp]assword:', pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT])` until EOF, appending\n  `child.before` on every iteration, then `\"\".join()` the pieces. Keep the password\n  pattern inside the loop so late prompts still match; treat success as the absence of\n  `Permission denied` in the joined text.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n\n### `qm guest exec` / `pct exec` / `pct push` only work on the OWNING node\n\n`/etc/pve` is cluster-readable, but guest-exec commands resolve the guest config against\nthe LOCAL node. Running `qm guest exec 103` from a node that doesn't own VM103 fails with\n`Configuration file 'nodes/<wrong-node>/qemu-server/103.conf' does not exist`. SSH to the\nowning node first (fleet map: VM103→miam-00111, VM401→miam-00112, VM109→miam-00143,\nVM111→miam-00144; CT115→miam-00147). `pct push` additionally requires the CT to be running.\n\n**PITFALL: `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -s` does NOT forward stdin.** A heredoc piped at\n`qm guest exec` feeds the node-side qm process, not the guest — the call returns\n`{\"exitcode\":0}` with EMPTY `out-data` while the guest script never ran. The reliable pattern is base64-in-command:\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -c \"echo <base64> | base64 -d | bash\"`.\nSame class of confusion: `scp node:/tmp/x` lands the file on the NODE — to move a file\nnode→CT use `pct push <ctid> /tmp/x /tmp/x` (and `pct pull` to fetch out).\n\n### Inspecting the GUEST vs the NODE (verify your vantage point)\n\nA script run directly through SSH lands on the NODE shell — its output (`pve-root` df,\nQEMU process list, host `lspci`, missing `nvidia-smi`) describes the hypervisor, NOT the\nguest. This burned a full diagnostic round: a \"bare guest\" verdict was drawn from node-shell\noutput while the guest was actually fully built (drivers + venv + model cache intact).\nRule: every guest-internal probe (cloud-init status, nvidia-smi, venv/model dirs, guest FS\nusage, guest internet) MUST be wrapped in `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc '...'` on the\nowning node. Before mutating anything based on an inspection, state which vantage point\nproduced the evidence.\n\n### Deploying a file INTO a VM guest when your box has no direct key\n\n1. base64 the deploy script locally; run it to the owning node over SSH, decode to /tmp there.\n2. From the node: `scp /tmp/x.sh <guest-user>@<guest-ip>:/tmp/` — the node holds the SSH\n   keypair baked into the guest's cloud-init `authorized_keys` (e.g. the `root@miam-00135`\n   RSA key referenced in cloud-config users).\n3. From the node: `ssh <guest-user>@<guest-ip> \"sudo bash /tmp/deploy.sh\"` — the cloud-init\n   user has NOPASSWD sudo. Direct SSH from your own box only works if YOUR key is in the\n   guest's authorized_keys; otherwise this node-relay path is the reliable route.\n\n### Debian 12 LXCs ship no sudo\n\n`sudo: command not found` inside a fresh Debian 12 CT. As root use\n`runuser -u <user> -- <cmd>` (e.g. `runuser -u postgres -- psql ...`). Locale/UTF-8 perl\nwarnings in these CTs are harmless noise.\n\n### Reconstructing an undocumented service into systemd from bash_history\n\nWhen a guest runs a critical service (e.g. vLLM) with no unit file, recover the final\nbattle-tested invocation from `grep -ihE '<service>' /root/.bash_history /home/*/.bash_history`\n—the LAST occurrence is the current working config. Turn it into\n`/etc/systemd/system/<svc>.service` (Environment lines for exports like HF_HOME, ExecStart\nwith all flags, Restart=on-failure, TimeoutStartSec generous for model load), then\n`daemon-reload && enable --now`, and verify via the service's HTTP endpoint\n(`/v1/models` + a small chat completion for vLLM) before calling it up.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up. Verified\n  working pattern: `pty.fork()` + `os.execvp('socat', ...)`, write `\\n`, then\n  `select()`-loop reads for ~20s; a booting/idle guest yields the `login:` banner.\n  Wrap it in a script landed on the node via base64-heredoc, then `python3 <script>`\n  — nodes have no pexpect installed (stdlib `pty`/`select` only).\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them.\n  ### Re-run WITHOUT recreating: bump the cloud-init instance-id\n  When a first boot half-applied (ssh/packages missing, wrong IP) you do NOT need to\n  destroy/rebuild: edit the user snippet (`/var/lib/vz/snippets/<name>.yaml`), bump\n  `instance-id` in the meta snippet (e.g. `bootstrap-v2` → `v3`), set static IP via\n  `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ip=<ip>/24,gw=<gw>`, then `qm stop <vmid> && qm start <vmid>`\n  (start regenerates the cloud-init ISO; the instance-id change forces cloud-init to\n  re-run per-instance modules). Poll readiness with `timeout 2 bash -c 'echo > /dev/tcp/<ip>/22'`\n  + `qm agent <vmid> ping`, then verify `cloud-init status --long` = done/no errors.\n  ### Static vs DHCP on vmbr0: VERIFY before asserting\n  Do NOT assume the LAN lacks DHCP — verified 2026-09 this vmbr0 /24 HAS a DHCP\n  server (a cloud-init `ip=dhcp` VM leased an address; all Hermes LXCs run `ip=dhcp`).\n  **After any build assigns statics, run the full audit in\n  `references/network-ip-collision-audit.md`** (asset-sheet MAC cross-check + ARP\n  classification + OPNsense DHCP pool boundary check via config.xml download) — the\n  user explicitly asks for this double-check, and it caught the corosync collision\n  below plus stale .115 references left in app configs.\n  Still assign STATIC IPs to infrastructure roles (managers/gateways/DB) so identities\n  never drift, but prove an address is free before assigning:\n  1) `qm list`/`pct list` per node + `pvesh get /cluster/nextid`\n  2) `grep -rE '10\\.0\\.20\\.[0-9]+' /etc/pve/qemu-server/ /etc/pve/lxc/`\n  3) `ip neigh show` + parallel ping sweep of the /24\n  4) **CLUSTER-WIDE: also check `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` `ring0_addr` values** — bare-metal\n  cluster nodes own

Iterating with `k.get(...)` throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n    Fetch per-key metadata with `GET /key/info?key=<token>` (query param, not JSON body);\n    the useful fields live under `info` (`key_alias`, `spend`, `max_budget`, `blocked`,\n    `expires`, `models`).\n  - Truncated 502 responses from `/key/generate` usually mean LiteLLM can't reach its Postgres — check\n  the litellm journal for `httpx.ConnectError` before touching app code. An unrepointed stale reference survived one\n  move and was only caught during a later network audit.\n- **Audit everything to a JSONL file** (`/var/log/llm-manager/audit.log`): login ok/denied +\n  reason, key issue/block/revoke, unit edits, restarts. The audit log is what makes\n  debugging auth failures possible (\"unknown user\" vs \"invalid credentials\" vs \"no role\"\n  each pinpoint a different broken layer).\n- **Guest control agent pattern** (no arbitrary shell): fixed-command script\n  `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh {status|get-unit|set-unit|restart|logs|nvidia}` per vLLM\n  guest; gateway host holds a dedicated SSH keypair (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/`),\n  pub key appended to each guest's `authorized_keys` via the owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec` (stdin is NOT forwarded — see proxmox skill). Unit edits: validate\n  `[Service]` + `ExecStart=` present, keep `.bak.<ts>`, `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restart —\n  the UI instructs the operator to restart separately (save ≠ apply).\n- **Keep the relay path documented for remote administration.** ssh_run.py (root+pexpect)\n  reaches Proxmox NODES only; the gateway VM takes a different path: land script on\n  owning node → `scp ubuntu@<vm-ip>` → `ssh … \"sudo bash /tmp/x.sh\"`. A session burned a\n  round-trip trying to ssh_run.py straight into the VM (`Permission denied (publickey)`).\n  Before driving a guest, check which path applies (node = root/password, VM =\n  node-relay with the baked-in key).\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- **Never invent vLLM flags.** Recover the battle-tested serve command from the target guest's root `~/.bash_history` (last working variant) and encode it in a systemd unit. Environment: `HF_HOME`, `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` are typical for this fleet.\n- **Per-deployment profiles.** The same logical model name can expose different `max_model_len` (observed 262144 vs 32768 on identical model IDs). Store concurrency/context envelopes per deployment; never assume homogeneity.\n- **Routable requires proof:** `/v1/models` returns the expected model AND a real chat completion succeeds — health endpoint alone is insufficient.\n- Qwen thinking models may emit reasoning inline unless `--reasoning-parser qwen3` is set — check parser flags when output contains \"Thinking Process...\". Thinking traces consume `max_tokens`: a \"max_tokens: 40\" test request can be fully consumed by the reasoning preamble before any answer text appears.\n- **Reasoning-parser fleet rollout pattern (verified 2026-09-10):** canary on ONE host first — add `--reasoning-parser qwen3` next to `--tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` in the unit, restart, verify `/v1/models` after the ~3-4 min reload, THEN confirm (a) `reasoning` field is separated and `content` is clean, (b) tool calling still works (parser combo must return proper `tool_calls`, not raw XML). Only then roll the rest, **staggered with per-host health gates** and automatic rollback if a host fails to come up in ~5 min. Unit NAMES differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — detect the RUNNING unit (`systemctl list-units --state=running | grep vllm`) instead of assuming; grep matching \"already set\" against the wrong unit file gives false positives. Clients get clean content via `\"chat_template_kwargs\": {\"enable_thinking\": false}` (e.g. `content: '27'` end-to-end through the gateway); with thinking ON, max_tokens is consumed by the reasoning preamble first.\n- **Hybrid-Mamba models (Qwen3.x family): `--max-num-seqs` must fit the Mamba cache.** Every concurrent sequence holds one Mamba cache block; 16 GB cards at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92` fit only ~92 blocks, so vLLM's default 256 aborts during CUDA-graph capture with `RuntimeError: max_num_seqs (256) exceeds available Mamba cache blocks (92)` and the unit crash-loops. Fix: `--max-num-seqs 64` (comfortable headroom, still generous for Hermes load). Block count scales with free VRAM per replica: dense Qwen3.8-27B at TP2/DP3 on 20 GB cards at 0.90 util had 163 blocks → `--max-num-seqs 128` succeeded after the default 256 aborted with the identical error. Never copy the 3080 hosts' turboquant KV/attention flags onto smaller-VRAM GPUs without qualification.\n- **llama.cpp as a serving engine (VM149, proven 2026-09-11):** the fleet is no longer\n  vLLM-only. llama.cpp `llama-server` (OpenAI-compatible :8000, `--alias <logical-name>`\n  sets the served model name) registers in LiteLLM the same way: `openai/<alias>` +\n  `api_base: http://<ip>:8000/v1` + dummy api_key. **Flag trap:** vLLM-style flags do\n  NOT exist in llama.cpp — `--mlock off` is invalid (`--mlock`/`--no-mlock` are the\n  llama.cpp forms, and no-mlock is already the default; one vLLM-habit flag caused a\n  9,740-restart crash-loop). Crash-loop from an invalid flag shows\n  `error: invalid argument: --<flag>` in journalctl within 1 s of each start — check\n  the unit's ExecStart against the ENGINE's flag set, not a generic one. Control agent\n  on such a host must be retargeted to the new unit name AND support full `set-unit`\n  (validate `[Service]`+`ExecStart=`, `.bak` backup, write, daemon-reload, NO\n  auto-restart — save ≠ apply), plus start/stop/version/models. Verify the agent's\n  get-unit/set-unit/restart roundtrip from VM114 before telling the user the UI works.\n- **Crash-loop signature:** systemd reports `active` while `NRestarts` climbs, worker PIDs change between checks, and nothing listens on :8000. Don't keep polling the port — pull `journalctl -u vllm --no-pager | grep -iE \"error|RuntimeError\"` f

Never prints secrets.\"\"\"\nimport json, ssl, sys, urllib.request, urllib.parse, os\n\nPVE = 'https://10.0.20.100:8006'\nTICKET_FILE = '/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/.pve_ticket'\nCTX = ssl.create_default_context(); CTX.check_hostname = False; CTX.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\n\n\ndef _login():\n    creds = open('/home/jordatech/.llm-manager-v011/creds/pve_root.txt').read().split()\n    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({'username': f'{creds[0]}@pam', 'password': creds[1]}).encode()\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + '/api2/json/access/ticket', data=data, method='POST')\n    r = json.load(urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=15))['data']\n    with open(TICKET_FILE, 'w') as f:\n        f.write(r['ticket'] + '\\n' + r['CSRFPreventionToken'])\n    os.chmod(TICKET_FILE, 0o600)\n\n\ndef _tickets():\n    try:\n        lines = open(TICKET_FILE).read().split('\\n')\n        return lines[0], lines[1]\n    except Exception:\n        _login()\n        return _tickets()\n\n\ndef api(method, path, body=None, retry=True):\n    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}'}\n    data = None\n    if body is not None:\n        enc = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode()\n        if method == 'GET':\n            # GET params must go in the querystring for PVE\n            path = path + ('&' if '?' in path else '?') + enc.decode()\n        else:\n            data = enc\n    if method != 'GET':\n        headers['CSRFPreventionToken'] = csrf\n    req = urllib.request.Request(PVE + path, data=data, headers=headers, method=method)\n    try:\n        resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=180)\n        return json.load(resp)\n    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:\n        if e.code == 401 and retry:\n            _login()\n            return api(method, path, body, retry=False)\n        raise\n\n\ndef find_vm(node_hint=None, vmid=None, ip=None):\n    \"\"\"Locate a VM/LXC by vmid or current IP (via guest agent if possible).\"\"\"\n    vms = []\n    nodes = [n['node'] for n in api('GET', '/api2/json/nodes')['data']]\n    for node in nodes:\n        for vm in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': vm['vmid'], 'name': vm.get('name', ''), 'status': vm['status'], 'type': 'qemu'})\n        for ct in api('GET', f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/lxc')['data']:\n            vms.append({'node': node, 'vmid': ct['vmid'], 'name': ct.get('name', ''), 'status': ct['status'], 'type': 'lxc'})\n    return vms\n\n\ndef guest_exec(node, vmid, command, timeout=120):\n    \"\"\"Run command in guest via qemu-guest-agent, wait for completion, return (stdout, stderr, exitcode).\n    PVE expects 'command' as repeated form fields (arg encode_array).\"\"\"\n    # PVE array convention: repeat the parameter name with each value\n    # NOTE: 'timeout' is NOT an accepted param for agent/exec (400s); the exec returns a pid\n    parts = []\n    for c in command:\n        parts.append('command=' + urllib.parse.quote(c, safe=''))\n    body = '&'.join(parts).encode()\n    url = PVE + f'/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec'\n    ticket, csrf = _tickets()\n    headers = {'Cookie': f'PVEAuthCookie={ticket}', 'CSRFPreventionToken': csrf,\n               'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded'}\n    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=body, headers=headers, method='POST')\n    resp = urllib.request.urlopen(req, context=CTX, timeout=timeout + 30)\n    pdata = json.load(resp)['data']", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

On hardware without native FP4 execution, AWQ W4A16 provides a practical\n   weight-only path while keeping activations in FP16.\n2. The routed-expert weights frequently have off-center local ranges, which an\n   asymmetric affine codebook can represent with a movable zero point.\n\nA route-frequency-stratified CPU audit covered all 93 layer/expert pairs with\nfewer than 128 natural tokens, including all eight zero-hit pairs, plus controls\nfrom every layer. In total it compared 707 layer/expert pairs, all three routed\nprojections, 2,121 matrices, and 69,500,928 BF16 weight values. It dequantized\nthe actual NVFP4 checkpoint and compared both formats against the same BF16\nmother weights; all 48 unquantized router tensors matched bitwise.\n\n| Route stratum | Pairs | Matrices | Actual NVFP4 relative RMSE | Affine g32 relative RMSE | Reduction | g32 wins |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|\n| Zero natural tokens | 8 | 24 | 0.094704 | 0.082320 | 13.08% | 24/24 |\n| 1–31 natural tokens | 14 | 42 | 0.094387 | 0.083253 | 11.80% | 42/42 |\n| 32–63 natural tokens | 17 | 51 | 0.094737 | 0.082029 | 13.41% | 51/51 |\n| 64–127 natural tokens | 54 | 162 | 0.094747 | 0.082104 | 13.34% | 162/162 |\n| Complete `<128` census | 93 | 279 | 0.094685 | 0.082289 | 13.09% | 279/279 |\n| Non-low-frequency controls | 614 | 1,842 | 0.094887 | 0.081588 | 14.02% | 1,842/1,842 |\n| All deduplicated selections | 707 | 2,121 | 0.094859 | 0.081686 | 13.89% | 2,121/2,121 |\n\nAffine g32 had lower error in every sampled matrix, including all 279\nlow-frequency gate/up/down matrices. The complete low-frequency census also\nreduced NMSE by 24.47%.\n\nThe result was not only a group-size effect: asymmetric affine g16 reached\n0.069948 relative RMSE, compared with 0.094859 for actual NVFP4. Of the sampled\nBF16 groups, 56.78% had a range-center shift above 0.1 and 25.00% above 0.2.\nOnly 55.40% of raw affine g32 zero-points and 58.73% of released AWQ zero-points\nlanded at the central 7/8 positions. These weights benefit from a movable\nasymmetric zero point rather than a fixed zero-centered codebook.\n\nThe g32 layout also matches the model's width-640 experts under TP4:\n`640 / 4 = 160`, and 160 is divisible by 32 but not 128. This avoids storing\ng128 metadata that must later be expanded to effective g32. The cost is about\n6.59 GiB more scale and zero-point metadata than g128.\n\n## Calibration coverage\n\nAWQ weight calibration used 684 records and 202,750 active tokens, producing\n97,320,000 native top-10 token-to-expert assignments across 48 layers. ### [2026-09-23T23:14:35.987038+00:00] assistant

**Status report — 18:12 CT (2h 48m to the 21:00 deadline)**

| Phase | Status |
|---|---|
| 1-2. Live capture + baseline rollback revisions | ✅ Done (5 baseline presets, ids 17-21) |
| 1b. Artifact fit gate | ✅ Caught 2 plan-killers before wasting hours: Flash-Next needs vLLM 0.30 (arch adapter missing in 0.28), VM103 needed +disk/+RAM |
| 3. Pre-change benchmarks (A/B/C/D ×5 hosts) | ✅ 14 runs stored in LLM Manager |
| 4. LLM Manager history/benchmark schema + API + UI card | ✅ Live, tested write+read |
| 5. 00111 PCIe capture | ✅ Clean (no AER storms); cold cycle deferred — it would kill the download & deployment |
| 7-8. **Gemma4 pool** | ✅ **LIVE on VM109+VM111**, 70K ctx, in `gemma4-26b-a4b` route, C8 = 76.2/62.7 t/s/agent (**~3× faster** than the qwen3.6 it replaced) |
| 9. VM401 qwen3.6 | ✅ Verified, untouched |
| 10. VM149 Qwen3.8-27B llama.cpp | ✅ Done as a **negative result**: measured 0.8 t/s (target 20) → restored Qwen3-4B; GGUF kept for later host upgrade |
| 6. Flash-Next on VM103 | ⚠️ **Fighting** — details below |

**Phase 6 (the hard one):** artifact (134GB) + vLLM 0.30 venv installed, TP2/DP3 confirmed as the only legal topology (TP must divide all head counts). Three blockers found and fixed in sequence: MoE assert (→ EP flag), HC-vs-seq-parallel-MoE conflict (→ patched 0.30's removed `naive` all2all backend back in), and now a guest **OOM during load** (52GB PLE table + 134GB model read flooding RAM). Currently on attempt with balloon disabled + memory tuning applied. **ETA for go/no-go: ~15 min** — the plan's own time box is nearly spent, so if this boot doesn't load, I **restore the qwen3.8-27b baseline on VM103** (10 min) per plan §9/§18, leaving a documented negative result.

**Guaranteed by 21:00:** full rollback revisions, every host either on its new model or restored known-good, final benchmark matrix + Hermes smoke tests + handoff doc. Continuing now.

| Host | Model | Result |
|---|---|---|

**The headline: the new Gemma pool is 2–3× faster than the qwen3.6 nodes it replaced** — C8 per-agent 76.2/62.7 tok/s (vs 22.6/29.2), aggregate 609/501 tok/s, 65K context passes, tool-calling correct. `gemma4-26b-a4b` is live in LiteLLM as one logical pool.

## Two negative results, both fully documented with reproduction chains
- **Flash-Next on the 3080s = blocked** (not by one thing — a chain: no Qwen4Exp adapter in vLLM 0.28 → built 0.30 venv; MoE width 640 fails TP2/DP3 without EP; EP triggers "HC does not support sequence-parallel MoE"; `naive` a2a was removed from 0.30 pip — patched back; then PLE = 17.5 GB shmem × 6 workers = 105 GB > 108 GB guest → OOM every boot). Artifact + venv are staged on VM103's new 250 GB disk for retry when upstream fixes Qwen4Exp's EP/PLE constraints.
- **Qwen3.8-27B on VM149 = 0.8 tok/s** (27B dense on CPU) → restored Qwen3-4B per your §14 rule. GGUF retained.

## LLM Manager (all live + verified)
New `deployment_revisions` table, extended `benchmark_runs`, `/api/history/*` endpoints, and a "Deployment history" dashboard card with a show-failed toggle. 5 exact rollback presets (ids 17–21). 24+ benchmark rows with revision linkage.

**Deferred (logged, not dropped):** 00111 cold-cycle (PCIe capture came back clean — no AER storms), gemma sticky-session routing, **OpenRouter key rotation** (surfaced in a DB dump), stale registry row cleanup.

- `p3_bench_summary.md` — final markdown summary with main table + A/B detail table (overwrote a stale partial version written by a concurrent process that had VM149 as "pending")
- `run_guest.py` — small wrapper around `pve.guest_exec` (see issue #1 below)

**Issues encountered:**
1. **pve.py exec timeout bug:** the CLI path uses the default `guest_exec(timeout=120)` and exposes no way to extend it — every bench would have died at 120s. I wrote `run_guest.py`, which imports `pve` and calls `guest_exec` with `timeout=1200`.
2. **VM111 + VM149 first-attempt subshell failures:** when launched as a paired `( ... ) & ( ... ) & wait` background job, those two subshells silently produced no output files (twice). Re-ran them as standalone background jobs, which worked. No root cause found; both hosts themselves were healthy (guest agent, bench.py, and python3 all verified reachable).
3. **VM149 D-32K HTTP 400:** recorded once and not retried per plan. Likely cause noted in summary: llama-server with `parallel=4` splits ctx 32768 into 4 ~8K slots, so a 32K prompt exceeds the per-slot limit. Its C1 throughput (15.1 t/s) is below the 20 t/s gate but it's the CPU box, outside the GPU fleet gate.
4. One diagnostic command was blocked by an approval gate (raw-IP URL pattern); skipped it rather than waiting.
5. Minor: 65K test on the 262K turboquant hosts returned 34/64 out-tokens — reasoning consumed part of the token budget; requests succeeded.
